Um… yeah, I came in because of work, mostly. I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Like when my route changes, they know before I even say anything, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio that sound casual, but they’re not.

People keep saying it’s just normal oversight, and I know there’s always tracking in transportation, I’m not stupid—I mean, my cousin used to say every job has somebody watching the clock, which is funny because he never kept a job—but this feels different. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.

Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.

I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things other people say, even when they seem normal, are actually about you or meant to send you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to explain. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 29 / transportation_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because dispatch and compliance have been tracking my route changes through the computer system and little comments on the radio, and even when people say it’s just normal oversight, I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.

Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I mean, I’m not saying there’s some device moving my hands or anything.

And certain drivers, I’m pretty sure, are reporting on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.

And people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.

I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Like when my route changes, they know before I even say anything, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio that sound casual, but they’re not.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just normal oversight, and I know there’s always tracking in transportation, I’m not stupid—I mean, my cousin used to say every job has somebody watching the clock, which is funny because he never kept a job—but this feels different. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages through the radio or other means, even if others say it's just normal communication?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nPeople keep saying, “That’s just compliance, that’s how transportation works,” and I get that. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things other people say, even when they seem normal, are actually about you or meant to send you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to explain. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. Not really TV, I don’t sit and watch much except sometimes cooking videos, and those are just cooking videos, you know. But with the route computer and the radio, it feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nLike dispatch will change something, or compliance will be looking at a route, and then there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds harmless, but the timing is too exact. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. Like if I read something online it’s just, you know, whatever people are arguing about. My cousin sends me those coupon posts and I don’t think that’s about me.\n\nIt’s more work. That’s why I’m here. The dispatch computer, the route changes, compliance watching it, and then these little radio comments right after—people say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are reporting back on me. I can tell. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. I don’t really watch it anyway—my mom always has those home renovation shows on, and half the time I’m just wondering why people pick white couches when they have kids.\n\nBut the thing I’m here about is work. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s too lined up to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people outside of work—like neighbors, strangers, or people you don’t know—are also watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Neighbors and strangers… I don’t know, not really like that. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.\n\nBecause the dispatch computer changes things, and compliance can see all that, and then right after I’ll hear these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but enough that I know certain drivers are reporting back on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it lines up too much. So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. I don’t think so. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I came in because of work, mostly. I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Like when my route changes, they know before I even say anything, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio that sound casual, but they’re not.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just normal oversight, and I know there’s always tracking in transportation, I’m not stupid—I mean, my cousin used to say every job has somebody watching the clock, which is funny because he never kept a job—but this feels different. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages through the radio or other means, even if others say it's just normal communication?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nPeople keep saying, “That’s just compliance, that’s how transportation works,” and I get that. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio after, like they’re acting casual but it’s too specific. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. Not really TV, I don’t sit and watch much except sometimes cooking videos, and those are just cooking videos, you know. But with the route computer and the radio, it feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nLike dispatch will change something, or compliance will be looking at a route, and then there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds harmless, but the timing is too exact. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. Like if I read something online it’s just, you know, whatever people are arguing about. My cousin sends me those coupon posts and I don’t think that’s about me.\n\nIt’s more work. That’s why I’m here. The dispatch computer, the route changes, compliance watching it, and then these little radio comments right after—people say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are reporting back on me. I can tell. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. I don’t really watch it anyway—my mom always has those home renovation shows on, and half the time I’m just wondering why people pick white couches when they have kids.\n\nBut the thing I’m here about is work. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s too lined up to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people outside of work—like neighbors, strangers, or people you don’t know—are also watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Neighbors and strangers… I don’t know, not really like that. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.\n\nBecause the dispatch computer changes things, and compliance can see all that, and then right after I’ll hear these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but enough that I know certain drivers are reporting back on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it lines up too much. So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. That sounds more like… I don’t know, like a movie or something.\n\nWhat I’m saying is more at work. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio after, like they’re acting casual but it’s too specific. And certain drivers, I’m pretty sure, are reporting on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I mean, I’m not saying there’s some device moving my hands or anything.\n\nBut the computer system at work, that’s the thing. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio after, like they’re pretending it’s just normal chatter. And it’s funny because my aunt used to say if something happens once, ignore it, twice, pay attention—she was always like that with coupons too, saving every receipt in this little envelope—but anyway, it keeps happening enough that I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. It’s mostly the work stuff.

People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that something you heard or saw—like a TV show, a conversation, or something online—was directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if it seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, but it’s the timing that makes it feel directed at me. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I came in because of work, mostly. I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Like when my route changes, they know before I even say anything, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio that sound casual, but they’re not.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just normal oversight, and I know there’s always tracking in transportation, I’m not stupid—I mean, my cousin used to say every job has somebody watching the clock, which is funny because he never kept a job—but this feels different. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages through the radio or other means, even if others say it's just normal communication?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nPeople keep saying, “That’s just compliance, that’s how transportation works,” and I get that. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things other people say, even when they seem normal, are actually about you or meant to send you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to explain. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. Not really TV, I don’t sit and watch much except sometimes cooking videos, and those are just cooking videos, you know. But with the route computer and the radio, it feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nLike dispatch will change something, or compliance will be looking at a route, and then there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds harmless, but the timing is too exact. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. Like if I read something online it’s just, you know, whatever people are arguing about. My cousin sends me those coupon posts and I don’t think that’s about me.\n\nIt’s more work. That’s why I’m here. The dispatch computer, the route changes, compliance watching it, and then these little radio comments right after—people say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are reporting back on me. I can tell. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. I don’t really watch it anyway—my mom always has those home renovation shows on, and half the time I’m just wondering why people pick white couches when they have kids.\n\nBut the thing I’m here about is work. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s too lined up to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people outside of work—like neighbors, strangers, or people you don’t know—are also watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Neighbors and strangers… I don’t know, not really like that. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.\n\nBecause the dispatch computer changes things, and compliance can see all that, and then right after I’ll hear these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but enough that I know certain drivers are reporting back on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it lines up too much. So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. That sounds more like… I don’t know, like a movie or something.\n\nWhat I’m saying is more at work. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio after, like they’re acting casual but it’s too specific. And certain drivers, I’m pretty sure, are reporting on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I mean, I’m not saying there’s some device moving my hands or anything.\n\nBut the computer system at work, that’s the thing. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio after, like they’re pretending it’s just normal chatter. And it’s funny because my aunt used to say if something happens once, ignore it, twice, pay attention—she was always like that with coupons too, saving every receipt in this little envelope—but anyway, it keeps happening enough that I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that something you heard or saw—like a TV show, a conversation, or something online—was directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if it seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. It’s mostly the work stuff.\n\nLike, if dispatch says something on the radio right after I change a route in the system, that’s when it stands out. Or compliance logs something and then suddenly certain drivers make these little comments like they already know. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, but it’s the timing that makes it feel directed at me. My uncle used to say if two things happen back-to-back, you should at least ask why—though he also thought the mailman skipped his house on purpose, so… I don’t know. But no, outside of work, I don’t really notice it that way.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that random events, like something you saw or heard, were specifically related to you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… no, not really like random TV or street signs or something. It’s more that I’m here because of what’s happening with dispatch and compliance.\n\nThey can see my route changes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did. People keep telling me it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell some of them are reporting on me. It’s not like everything in the world is about me—it’s that system, those people, the timing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really outside of work, no. I mean… I guess if someone says something at work, that’s different, because dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer. Then there’ll be some little comment on the radio, or a driver says something sideways, and it’s like—how would they know that unless somebody told them?

But, like, if I’m at a restaurant or my cousin says something at a barbecue, I don’t usually think it’s secretly about me. My cousin talks in circles anyway, so half the time nobody knows what she means. But with work, it’s not random. I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages through the radio or other means, even if others say it's just normal communication?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nPeople keep saying, “That’s just compliance, that’s how transportation works,” and I get that. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things other people say, even when they seem normal, are actually about you or meant to send you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to explain. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. Not really TV, I don’t sit and watch much except sometimes cooking videos, and those are just cooking videos, you know. But with the route computer and the radio, it feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nLike dispatch will change something, or compliance will be looking at a route, and then there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds harmless, but the timing is too exact. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. Like if I read something online it’s just, you know, whatever people are arguing about. My cousin sends me those coupon posts and I don’t think that’s about me.\n\nIt’s more work. That’s why I’m here. I can tell. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. I don’t really watch it anyway—my mom always has those home renovation shows on, and half the time I’m just wondering why people pick white couches when they have kids.\n\nBut the thing I’m here about is work. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s too lined up to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people outside of work—like neighbors, strangers, or people you don’t know—are also watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Neighbors and strangers… I don’t know, not really like that. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.\n\nBecause the dispatch computer changes things, and compliance can see all that, and then right after I’ll hear these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but enough that I know certain drivers are reporting back on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it lines up too much. So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. That sounds more like… I don’t know, like a movie or something.\n\nWhat I’m saying is more at work. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio after, like they’re acting casual but it’s too specific. And certain drivers, I’m pretty sure, are reporting on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I mean, I’m not saying there’s some device moving my hands or anything.\n\nBut the computer system at work, that’s the thing. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio after, like they’re pretending it’s just normal chatter. And it’s funny because my aunt used to say if something happens once, ignore it, twice, pay attention—she was always like that with coupons too, saving every receipt in this little envelope—but anyway, it keeps happening enough that I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that something you heard or saw—like a TV show, a conversation, or something online—was directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if it seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. It’s mostly the work stuff.\n\nLike, if dispatch says something on the radio right after I change a route in the system, that’s when it stands out. Or compliance logs something and then suddenly certain drivers make these little comments like they already know. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, but it’s the timing that makes it feel directed at me. My uncle used to say if two things happen back-to-back, you should at least ask why—though he also thought the mailman skipped his house on purpose, so… I don’t know. But no, outside of work, I don’t really notice it that way.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that random events, like something you saw or heard, were specifically related to you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… no, not really like random TV or street signs or something. It’s more that I’m here because of what’s happening with dispatch and compliance.\n\nThey can see my route changes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did. People keep telling me it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell some of them are reporting on me. It’s not like everything in the world is about me—it’s that system, those people, the timing.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that things people said or did were secretly about you or meant something personal, even if they didn’t seem important to others?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean… I guess if someone says something at work, that’s different, because dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer. Then there’ll be some little comment on the radio, or a driver says something sideways, and it’s like—how would they know that unless somebody told them?\n\nBut, like, if I’m at a restaurant or my cousin says something at a barbecue, I don’t usually think it’s secretly about me. My cousin talks in circles anyway, so half the time nobody knows what she means. But with work, it’s not random. People say, “That’s just oversight,” but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s the timing. That’s why I’m here, really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Outside of work, not so much. I don’t usually think, like, a neighbor coughing or somebody posting online is about me.

It’s not like everyone at work. But dispatch and compliance, they can see when I change my route in the computer, and then later there’ll be these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but close enough that I know it’s connected. And some drivers, certain ones, they act normal if you’re just looking at them, but then they say something that makes it obvious they heard about what I did.

People keep saying it’s just oversight, and I get that transportation has rules—I’m not saying there shouldn’t be rules. My supervisor is always talking about efficiency too, like when my sister was telling me her job has tracking for deliveries, but that’s not the same thing. With me, the timing is too exact. So yes, I do think some of them are reporting on me, even if other people don’t see it.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22.0, F20.0, F20.1, F20.2, F20.3, F21\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I came in because of work, mostly. I drive routes, and lately dispatch and compliance have been… tracking me, I guess, through the computer system. Like when my route changes, they know before I even say anything, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio that sound casual, but they’re not.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just normal oversight, and I know there’s always tracking in transportation, I’m not stupid—I mean, my cousin used to say every job has somebody watching the clock, which is funny because he never kept a job—but this feels different. Certain drivers are reporting on me, I can tell. It’s like they’re all comparing notes.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like people are talking about you or trying to send you messages through the radio or other means, even if others say it's just normal communication?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, yes, kind of. It’s mostly through the dispatch computer and the radio. Like my route changes and somehow they’re already reacting to it, and then someone says something over the radio that sounds normal, like traffic or timing, but it feels pointed at me.\n\nPeople keep saying, “That’s just compliance, that’s how transportation works,” and I get that. I know there’s tracking. But this is different. Certain drivers are reporting back about me, I can tell. Like they’re comparing notes. And it’s funny, because one of them always acts friendly in the break room, talking about his kid’s soccer schedule, and I’m standing there thinking, okay, but what did you tell them about my route?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things other people say, even when they seem normal, are actually about you or meant to send you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s what I’m trying to explain. Like, the words sound normal if you just write them down, but it’s the timing. Dispatch changes something in the computer, and then the radio has these little comments, like about “staying on route” or “keeping things clean,” and it’s like… okay, that’s not random.\n\nAnd certain drivers, I can tell they’re reporting back about me. They’ll act like they’re just chatting, but then compliance seems to know things. People say it’s normal oversight, and I know jobs track stuff, especially routes—I mean, my aunt used to work in payroll and she’d say everybody thinks payroll is spying when really they’re just fixing time cards—but this is different. It feels meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things happening around you—like things people say, or things you see on TV or the internet—are secretly about you or meant to give you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly with work stuff. Not really TV, I don’t sit and watch much except sometimes cooking videos, and those are just cooking videos, you know. But with the route computer and the radio, it feels like it’s aimed at me.\n\nLike dispatch will change something, or compliance will be looking at a route, and then there’s a little comment over the radio that sounds harmless, but the timing is too exact. People say it’s just oversight, normal transportation stuff, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. I can tell they’re passing things along.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—are meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seem normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. Like if I read something online it’s just, you know, whatever people are arguing about. My cousin sends me those coupon posts and I don’t think that’s about me.\n\nIt’s more work. That’s why I’m here. The dispatch computer, the route changes, compliance watching it, and then these little radio comments right after—people say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are reporting back on me. I can tell. They’ll act casual, like they’re just talking, but it lines up too much.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt like things happening around you—like something you hear, see, or read—were meant for you or sending you a message, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside of work, not so much. Like, I don’t think the TV is talking to me or anything like that. I don’t really watch it anyway—my mom always has those home renovation shows on, and half the time I’m just wondering why people pick white couches when they have kids.\n\nBut the thing I’m here about is work. Dispatch and compliance are tracking my route changes through the computer system, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio right after. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, like it’s just how transportation works, but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s too lined up to be nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people outside of work—like neighbors, strangers, or people you don’t know—are also watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Neighbors and strangers… I don’t know, not really like that. I mean, sometimes people look at you weird at the store, but people look weird at everybody, right? Like last week this guy was staring at my cart because I had three kinds of cereal, which—my mom likes one kind, I like another, it’s not a big thing.\n\nBut the main thing is work. That’s where it’s happening. Dispatch and compliance are watching my route changes through the computer, and then right after, there’ll be these little radio comments like they’re pretending it’s casual. People say it’s normal oversight, but certain drivers are definitely reporting on me. That’s the part I can’t ignore.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions at work are being controlled or influenced by something outside of you, like the computer system or the people watching your route?\n\nPatient: Controlled… I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. Like, I’m still the one driving the route and making the turns, you know? But it does feel like they’re trying to push me a certain way.\n\nBecause the dispatch computer changes things, and compliance can see all that, and then right after I’ll hear these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but enough that I know certain drivers are reporting back on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it lines up too much. So… influenced, maybe. Not like a robot or anything, but like they’re steering things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like thoughts are being put into your mind, taken out of your mind, or broadcast from your mind that you don’t control?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think thoughts are being put in my head or taken out. That sounds more like… I don’t know, like a movie or something.\n\nWhat I’m saying is more at work. Like dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio after, like they’re acting casual but it’s too specific. And certain drivers, I’m pretty sure, are reporting on me. People keep saying that’s just normal oversight, but it doesn’t feel normal when it keeps lining up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts or actions are being directly controlled by something outside of yourself, like a person, device, or system?\n\nPatient: Directly controlled… no, I wouldn’t say it like that exactly. I mean, I’m not saying there’s some device moving my hands or anything.\n\nBut the computer system at work, that’s the thing. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio after, like they’re pretending it’s just normal chatter. And it’s funny because my aunt used to say if something happens once, ignore it, twice, pay attention—she was always like that with coupons too, saving every receipt in this little envelope—but anyway, it keeps happening enough that I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that something you heard or saw—like a TV show, a conversation, or something online—was directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if it seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. It’s mostly the work stuff.\n\nLike, if dispatch says something on the radio right after I change a route in the system, that’s when it stands out. Or compliance logs something and then suddenly certain drivers make these little comments like they already know. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, but it’s the timing that makes it feel directed at me. My uncle used to say if two things happen back-to-back, you should at least ask why—though he also thought the mailman skipped his house on purpose, so… I don’t know. But no, outside of work, I don’t really notice it that way.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that random events, like something you saw or heard, were specifically related to you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… no, not really like random TV or street signs or something. It’s more that I’m here because of what’s happening with dispatch and compliance.\n\nThey can see my route changes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did. People keep telling me it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell some of them are reporting on me. It’s not like everything in the world is about me—it’s that system, those people, the timing.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that things people said or did were secretly about you or meant something personal, even if they didn’t seem important to others?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean… I guess if someone says something at work, that’s different, because dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer. Then there’ll be some little comment on the radio, or a driver says something sideways, and it’s like—how would they know that unless somebody told them?\n\nBut, like, if I’m at a restaurant or my cousin says something at a barbecue, I don’t usually think it’s secretly about me. My cousin talks in circles anyway, so half the time nobody knows what she means. But with work, it’s not random. People say, “That’s just oversight,” but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s the timing. That’s why I’m here, really.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that things people said or did—like a comment, action, or event—were specifically about you or meant something personal, even if others didn’t seem to think so?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not so much. I don’t usually think, like, a neighbor coughing or somebody posting online is about me.\n\nBut at work, yes—that’s why I’m here. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then these little comments come over the radio, or certain drivers say things like they already know what I did. People tell me it’s normal oversight, but the timing is too exact. I can tell some drivers are reporting on me.\n\nIt’s not everything, you know? My aunt will say weird things at dinner, but she’s always been like that. This is more specific—work, the system, the route changes. That’s what feels personal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people at work were working together secretly to observe or report on you, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s exactly what I’ve been trying to explain, but it sounds bad when I say it like that.\n\nIt’s not like everyone at work. But dispatch and compliance, they can see when I change my route in the computer, and then later there’ll be these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but close enough that I know it’s connected. And some drivers, certain ones, they act normal if you’re just looking at them, but then they say something that makes it obvious they heard about what I did.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just oversight, and I get that transportation has rules—I’m not saying there shouldn’t be rules. My supervisor is always talking about efficiency too, like when my sister was telling me her job has tracking for deliveries, but that’s not the same thing. With me, the timing is too exact. So yes, I do think some of them are reporting on me, even if other people don’t see it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Outside of work… not really. Like, if I hear a song in a store or somebody laughs behind me, I don’t usually think it’s for me or anything.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, at work, yes. That’s the part people don’t seem to get.

Like, I’ll change something in the route system—nothing dramatic, just a route adjustment—and then later dispatch or compliance will say something on the radio that sounds normal to everyone else, but I know it’s about that. It’s the timing. And certain drivers will make these little comments too, like they already heard about it.

I know people say, “Well, that’s just oversight,” and I understand jobs track things. My cousin’s workplace has cameras all over, actually, and she says you stop noticing them after a while, but this isn’t just that. This feels more specific to me. Like they’re reporting on me and then talking around it, so it sounds normal if someone else is listening.
