Dispatch and compliance can see when I change my route, and then there’ll be these little comments on the radio after, like they’re pretending it’s just normal chatter. And it’s funny because my aunt used to say if something happens once, ignore it, twice, pay attention—she was always like that with coupons too, saving every receipt in this little envelope—but anyway, it keeps happening enough that I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. People call it normal oversight, but it feels more targeted than that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that your thoughts were being inserted into your mind, removed from it, or broadcast to others without your control?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t walk around feeling like people can hear my thoughts or like somebody’s taking them out of my head.\n\nSometimes I get distracted, like I’ll go into the kitchen and forget why I’m there, but that’s just… everybody does that, right? My mom used to say it’s because I’m trying to do five things at once, and she’d be chopping onions and answering the phone and then blame the onions for everything. But no, not like thoughts being broadcast or inserted. I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you heard, like a phrase over the radio or a comment from someone, had a special meaning for you that other people wouldn't understand, even if they thought it was just normal conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what I’ve been trying to explain. Like over the radio, they’ll say something that sounds normal to everybody else, like a route comment or some little joke, but I know it’s about me because I had just changed something in the computer.\n\nAnd people say, “Oh, dispatch can see routes, that’s their job,” but it’s not just that. It’s the timing. Compliance sees it, dispatch sees it, then certain drivers start acting like they know. Like they’re reporting back. My aunt used to say timing tells you more than words—she was always saying that about neighbors, too, like if someone mowed their lawn right after she complained—but… sorry. Yes, sometimes the comments feel like they have a meaning for me, even if other people act like it’s just normal work talk.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations not related to work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no… I mean, most of what I notice is tied to the routes and the computer system. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change something, and then there are these little radio comments, or certain drivers act like they know already.\n\nIf I’m at the grocery store or with family or something, I don’t usually think, “That’s a message for me.” It’s more when the work system is involved. People say it’s normal oversight, but the way it lines up, it feels like they’re tracking me and reporting on me.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever felt that something you heard or saw—like a TV show, a conversation, or something online—was directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if it seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really… I don’t sit at home watching TV thinking they’re talking to me or anything like that. It’s mostly the work stuff.\n\nLike, if dispatch says something on the radio right after I change a route in the system, that’s when it stands out. Or compliance logs something and then suddenly certain drivers make these little comments like they already know. People keep saying it’s normal oversight, but it’s the timing that makes it feel directed at me. My uncle used to say if two things happen back-to-back, you should at least ask why—though he also thought the mailman skipped his house on purpose, so… I don’t know. But no, outside of work, I don’t really notice it that way.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that random events, like something you saw or heard, were specifically related to you, even if others didn’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… no, not really like random TV or street signs or something. It’s more that I’m here because of what’s happening with dispatch and compliance.\n\nThey can see my route changes in the computer, and then there’ll be these little comments over the radio, or certain drivers act like they already know what I did. People keep telling me it’s just normal oversight, but I can tell some of them are reporting on me. It’s not like everything in the world is about me—it’s that system, those people, the timing.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that things people said or did were secretly about you or meant something personal, even if they didn’t seem important to others?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of work, no. I mean… I guess if someone says something at work, that’s different, because dispatch and compliance can see my route changes in the computer. Then there’ll be some little comment on the radio, or a driver says something sideways, and it’s like—how would they know that unless somebody told them?\n\nBut, like, if I’m at a restaurant or my cousin says something at a barbecue, I don’t usually think it’s secretly about me. My cousin talks in circles anyway, so half the time nobody knows what she means. But with work, it’s not random. People say, “That’s just oversight,” but I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It’s the timing. That’s why I’m here, really.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that things people said or did—like a comment, action, or event—were specifically about you or meant something personal, even if others didn’t seem to think so?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not so much. I don’t usually think, like, a neighbor coughing or somebody posting online is about me.\n\nBut at work, yes—that’s why I’m here. Dispatch and compliance can see when I change routes in the computer, and then these little comments come over the radio, or certain drivers say things like they already know what I did. People tell me it’s normal oversight, but the timing is too exact. I can tell some drivers are reporting on me.\n\nIt’s not everything, you know? My aunt will say weird things at dinner, but she’s always been like that. This is more specific—work, the system, the route changes. That’s what feels personal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people at work were working together secretly to observe or report on you, even if they seemed normal to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s exactly what I’ve been trying to explain, but it sounds bad when I say it like that.\n\nIt’s not like everyone at work. But dispatch and compliance, they can see when I change my route in the computer, and then later there’ll be these little comments on the radio. Not direct, like “we’re watching you,” but close enough that I know it’s connected. And some drivers, certain ones, they act normal if you’re just looking at them, but then they say something that makes it obvious they heard about what I did.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just oversight, and I get that transportation has rules—I’m not saying there shouldn’t be rules. My supervisor is always talking about efficiency too, like when my sister was telling me her job has tracking for deliveries, but that’s not the same thing. With me, the timing is too exact. So yes, I do think some of them are reporting on me, even if other people don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever believed that neutral events, like something you saw or heard, were personally directed at you or had a special meaning for you, even if others didn't notice anything unusual?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really. Like, if I hear a song in a store or somebody laughs behind me, I don’t usually think it’s for me or anything.\n\nIt’s more the work stuff. That’s why I keep coming back to dispatch and compliance, because they actually can see my route changes in the computer. And then there’ll be these little comments on the radio, or a driver will say something like they already know what happened. People say it’s just normal oversight, and I know every job has systems—my friend works in a warehouse and they scan everything there too—but with me it’s the timing. It feels directed. Like certain drivers are reporting on me, even if everyone else acts like it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or conversations at work had a special meaning for you that others wouldn't understand, even if they seemed normal to everyone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, at work, yes. That’s the part people don’t seem to get.\n\nLike, I’ll change something in the route system—nothing dramatic, just a route adjustment—and then later dispatch or compliance will say something on the radio that sounds normal to everyone else, but I know it’s about that. It’s the timing. And certain drivers will make these little comments too, like they already heard about it.\n\nI know people say, “Well, that’s just oversight,” and I understand jobs track things. My cousin’s workplace has cameras all over, actually, and she says you stop noticing them after a while, but this isn’t just that. This feels more specific to me. Like they’re reporting on me and then talking around it, so it sounds normal if someone else is listening."
